\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{1}\right)\frac{((\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{\dagger}y_{D}^{\ast})_{ps}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}y_{E}-y_{U}^{\ast}\lambda^{1\text{R}})_{tr}}{M_{1}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{2}\right)\frac{(\tilde{\lambda}^{\dagger}y_{D}^{\dagger})_{pt}(\tilde{\lambda}y_{E})_{sr}}{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}\;,

(3.162)

\displaystyle\left[G^{(1)}_{\ell equ}\right]^{(1)}_{prst}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{1+N_{c}}{2}\left(1+L_{1}\right)c_{1}+\frac{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\left(1+L_{2}\right)\left(N_{c}c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}}\right)+8|A^{\prime}|^{2}\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\,L_{2}\right]\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pt})^{\dagger}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{sr})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{1}\right)\left[g_{s}^{2}C_{F}^{\text{SU(3)}}+g^{\prime 2}(Y_{q}-Y_{\ell})(Y_{u}-Y_{e})\right]\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{ps})^{\dagger}\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{tr}}{M_{1}^{2}}\;,

(3.163)

\displaystyle\left[G^{(3)}_{\ell equ}\right]^{(1)}_{prst}

\displaystyle=-\left[\frac{1+N_{c}}{8}\left(1+L_{1}\right)c_{1}+\frac{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}{4M_{1}^{2}}\left(1+L_{2}\right)\left(N_{c}c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}}\right)+8|A^{\prime}|^{2}\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\,L_{2}\right]\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pt})^{\dagger}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{sr})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{1}\right)\left[g_{s}^{2}C_{F}^{\text{SU(3)}}+g^{\prime 2}(Y_{q}-Y_{\ell})(Y_{u}-Y_{e})\right]\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{ps})^{\dagger}\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{tr}}{M_{1}^{2}}\;.

(3.164)

B-violating

\displaystyle\left[G_{duq}\right]^{(1)}_{prst}

\displaystyle=\left[\left(1+N_{c}\right)\left(1+L_{1}\right)c_{1}+\frac{2\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\left(1+L_{2}\right)\left(N_{c}c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}}\right)+8|A^{\prime}|^{2}\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\,L_{2}\right]\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left(1+L_{1}\right)\left[g^{\prime 2}\left(Y_{d}(Y_{q}-Y_{S_{1}})+Y_{u}(Y_{\ell}-Y_{S_{1}})+Y_{d}(Y_{u}-Y_{S_{1}})\right)\right]\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{1}{2}+a_{ev}\right)(Y_{\ell}+Y_{u})(Y_{u}+Y_{d})\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+g_{s}^{2}\left[\frac{4}{3}\left(\frac{1}{2}+a_{ev}\right)-\frac{13}{6}(1+L_{1})\right]\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{((\tilde{\lambda})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{T})_{ps}((\lambda^{\not{B}R})^{\dagger}\tilde{\lambda})_{rt}}{M_{1}^{2}-\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}\log\frac{M_{1}^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}}y_{E}+y_{U}^{\ast}\lambda^{1\text{R}})_{pt}((\lambda^{\not{B}R})^{\dagger}y_{D}^{\dagger}+2y_{U}^{T}(\lambda^{\not{B}L})^{\ast})_{sr}}{2M_{1}^{2}}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{1}\right)\;,

(3.165)

\displaystyle\left[G_{qqu}\right]^{(1)}_{prst}

\displaystyle=\left[\left(1+N_{c}\right)\left(1+L_{1}\right)c_{1}+\frac{2\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\left(1+L_{2}\right)\left(N_{c}c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}}\right)+8|A^{\prime}|^{2}\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\,L_{2}\right]\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\left(1+L_{1}\right)\left[4g^{\prime 2}Y_{S_{1}}(2Y_{q}+Y_{S_{1}})\right]\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{st})}{4M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+2g^{\prime 2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+a_{ev}\right)Y_{q}(Y_{e}-Y_{u})\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+g_{s}^{2}\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}\left[\frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{1}{2}+a_{ev}\right)-\frac{5}{2}(1+L_{1})\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{(2(\lambda^{\not{B}L})^{\ast}y_{D}-y_{U}^{\ast}(\lambda^{\not{B}R})^{\dagger})_{sp}(y_{E}^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{R}})^{T}-(\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{T}y_{U})_{tr}}{2M_{1}^{2}}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{(y_{D}^{T}\lambda^{1\text{L}})_{pt}(2(\lambda^{\not{B}L})^{\ast}y_{U}+y_{D}^{\ast}(\lambda^{\not{B}R})^{\ast})_{sr}}{2M_{1}^{2}}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{1}\right)\;,

(3.166)

\displaystyle\left[G_{qqq}\right]^{(1)}_{prst}

\displaystyle=-\left[\left(1+N_{c}\right)\left(1+L_{1}\right)c_{1}+\frac{2\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\left(1+L_{2}\right)\left(N_{c}c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}}\right)+8|A^{\prime}|^{2}\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\,L_{2}\right]\frac{2(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left(1+L_{1}\right)\left[2g^{\prime 2}Y_{S_{1}}(6Y_{q}+Y_{\ell})\right]\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{st})}{2M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle+g_{s}^{2}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{1}\right)\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{ps})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{rt})}{12M_{1}^{2}}-\frac{5}{3}g_{s}^{2}\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}\left(-\frac{3}{5}+L_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2g^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{1}\right)\left[2(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{ps})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{rt})+(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{rs})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{tp})-(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{st})\right]\;,

(3.167)

\displaystyle\left[G_{duu}\right]^{(1)}_{prst}

\displaystyle=\left[\left(1+N_{c}\right)\left(1+L_{1}\right)c_{1}+\frac{2\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\left(1+L_{2}\right)\left(N_{c}c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}}\right)+8|A^{\prime}|^{2}\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\,L_{2}\right]\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\left[4\left(1+L_{1}\right)g^{\prime 2}(2Y_{u}^{2}-2Y_{e}Y_{d}-Y_{e}Y_{S_{1}})\right.

\displaystyle+\left.2g^{\prime 2}Y_{u}(Y_{d}+3Y_{u})-2g^{\prime 2}Y_{e}(3Y_{d}-Y_{u})\right]\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{st})}{4M_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle-2g^{\prime 2}\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{ps})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{rt})}{M_{1}^{2}}\left(\frac{3}{2}+L_{1}\right)\left[Y_{d}(Y_{e}-Y_{u})-Y_{u}(Y_{e}+Y_{u})\right]

\displaystyle-g_{s}^{2}\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{7}{3}+L_{1}\right)\frac{(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{st})}{M_{1}^{2}}\;.

(3.168)

In total, 109 out of 139 operators are generated in the Green basis which translates into 53 out of 59 operators in the Warsaw basis. The only operators not generated by the
S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2}
-model
in the Warsaw basis are the CP violating ones, namely
Q_{3(\tilde{G},\tilde{W}),H(\tilde{B},\tilde{W},\tilde{G}),H\tilde{W}B}
, which are of course absent in the Green basis as well.

The UOLEA parameters
\tilde{f}_{11}-\tilde{f}_{19}
appearing in Vector-Bosons-Scalar operators are given separately in Appendix
D
. The hypercharges of the SM chiral fermions and the Higgs are,

Y_{\ell}=-\frac{1}{2}\;,\quad Y_{e}=-1\;,\quad Y_{q}=\frac{1}{6}\;,\quad Y_{u}=\frac{2}{3}\;,\quad Y_{d}=-\frac{1}{3}\;,\quad{\rm and}\quad Y_{H}=\frac{1}{2}\;,

(3.169)

respectively, while
Y_{S_{1}}=1/3
and
Y_{\tilde{S}_{2}}=1/6
for leptoquarks.

3.4
Theoretical Remarks

Further remarks on our findings for the complete set of
d\leq 6
Wilson coefficients in Green basis at one-loop are in order.

3.4.1
Evanescent Operators

Evanescent operators appear in 4-point functions involving fermions. Treating the integrals in
d
-dimensions while using Fierz identities, that hold only in
d=4
or encountering higher order of
\gamma
-matrices products, give rise to evanescent operators that in general vanish in
d=4
. The scheme we will be using for this type of structures is the introduction of local counterterms
a_{ev},\ldots,f_{ev}
. For details the reader is referred to refs.
BURAS199066
;
Herrlich:1994kh
;
Dugan:1990df
;
Aebischer:2020dsw
;
Dekens:2019ept
.

Although, strictly-speaking, not part of the actual matching calculation, in translating the raw results of the traces after substituting the
U
matrices, one needs to choose a definite scheme to reduce the
\gamma
-matrix structure appearing in the subsequent equations and match it to a certain basis, such as the Green basis. In the model under consideration evanescent operators make their appearance in the Supertrace of (
2.35
) where both left and right projection operators appear in the same trace. We keep a general parameter
a_{ev}
not resorting to any particular scheme. The usual scheme choice for evanescent operators, however, is
a_{ev}=\ldots=f_{ev}=1
. The relevant Dirac-structures appearing in the model at hand are (in the NDR scheme
d=4-\epsilon
),

\displaystyle P_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{\nu}P_{L}\otimes P_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}P_{L}

\displaystyle=4\left(1-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)\,P_{L}\otimes P_{L}-P_{L}\sigma_{\mu\nu}P_{L}\otimes P_{L}\sigma^{\mu\nu}P_{L}\;,

(3.170)

\displaystyle P_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{\nu}P_{L}\otimes P_{L}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}

\displaystyle=4\left(1-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)\,P_{L}\otimes P_{L}+P_{L}\sigma_{\mu\nu}P_{L}\otimes P_{L}\sigma^{\mu\nu}P_{L}\;,

(3.171)

\displaystyle P_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}P_{L}\otimes P_{R}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}P_{R}

\displaystyle=4\left(1+a_{ev}\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\,P_{L}\otimes P_{R}+E^{(2)}_{LR}\;,

(3.172)

\displaystyle P_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}P_{L}\otimes P_{R}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{R}

\displaystyle=4\left[1-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\left(1+a_{ev}\right)\right]\,P_{L}\otimes P_{R}+E^{(2)}_{LR}\;.

(3.173)

Plugging in a specific value for the coefficient
a_{ev}
, in our case, defines the evanescent operators
E^{(2)}_{LR}
.

3.4.2
RGE checks

As a further cross check of our results for Wilson coefficients we have calculated the Renormalization Group Equations (RGEs) for a certain set. For purely one-loop generated operators one has to just take the derivative with respect to the renormalization scale
\mu
and extract the relevant
\beta
-function. For instance explicitly taking the derivative with respect to
\mu
on
[C_{eW}]_{pr}
we find,

\displaystyle\frac{d[C_{eW}]_{pr}}{d\ln\mu}=-N_{c}\ \frac{g}{4}\ \frac{(Y^{1L}_{1U})_{pr}}{M_{1}^{2}}\;.

(3.174)

Comparing with the
\beta
-functions from
Jenkins:2013zja
;
Jenkins:2013wua
;
Alonso:2013hga
;
Celis:2017hod
,

\displaystyle[\beta_{eW}]_{pr}=6\ g\ (y_{U})^{\ast}_{st}\ \left[C_{\ell equ}^{(3)}\right]_{prst}\;,

(3.175)

after plugging in the value of the relevant Wc [eq. (
3.14
)], we get,

\displaystyle[\beta_{eW}]_{pr}=-\frac{3g}{4}\ \frac{(Y^{1L}_{1U})_{pr}}{M_{1}^{2}}\;,

(3.176)

which is in complete agreement, for
N_{c}=3
, with the direct application of the derivative. The same procedure has been followed for every other purely one-loop generated Wcs and we have found no discrepancies in the comparison.

For operators generated at tree-level as well the picture is a bit different because at
tree-level the coupling of the respective Wcs must be considered as running parameters due to shifts of the corresponding fields. However these exact shifts will cancel with the RG running due to wavefunction renormalization. For example, to bring back the lepton and quark doublet kinetic term we must make the following shift,

\displaystyle\ell_{p}\

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ \ell_{p}-\frac{1}{2}\ (\delta Z_{\ell})_{pp_{1}}\ \ell_{p_{1}}\;,

(3.177)

\displaystyle q_{p}\

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ q_{p}-\frac{1}{2}\ (\delta Z_{q})_{pp_{1}}\ q_{p_{1}}\;,

(3.178)

where
\delta Z_{(\ell,q)}
correspond to eqs. (
3.43
) and (
3.45
), respectively. We have also suppressed all other indices apart from generation indices.
In turn this produces a shift in the coupling
\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr}
. To absorb this shift we redefine the coupling as,

\displaystyle(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr})^{\text{eff.}}=\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr}-\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pw}\ (\delta Z_{\ell})_{wr}-\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{wr}\ (\delta Z_{q})_{wp}\;.

(3.179)

Taking the derivative w.r.t
\mu
-parameter we find the
\beta
-function for the effective running coupling,

\displaystyle[\beta_{\lambda^{1\text{L}}}]_{pr}=\

\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{2}\ \left[\lambda^{1\text{L}}(\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{\dagger}\lambda^{1\text{L}}+\lambda^{1\text{L}}(\tilde{\lambda})^{\dagger}\tilde{\lambda}\right]_{pr}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\ \left[(\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{T}(\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{T}-8(\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{T}\lambda^{\not{B}L}(\lambda^{\not{B}L})^{\dagger}\right]_{pr}\;.

(3.180)

The same of course can be done for the complex conjugate coupling,

\displaystyle[\beta_{(\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{\dagger}}]_{pr}=

\displaystyle\ [\beta_{\lambda^{1\text{L}}}]^{\dagger}_{pr}\;.

(3.181)

These two will contribute, for instance, in the RG evolution of the operator
C_{\ell q}^{(1)}
. Ignoring finite parts, and for the clarity of the cancellation considering only the part produced by the shifts in the fermion fields, we find

\displaystyle[C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]_{prst}\propto[C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{prst}-\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\left\{(\delta Z_{\ell})_{pw}\ [C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{wrst}+(\delta Z_{\ell})_{rw}\ [C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{pwst}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(\delta Z_{q})_{sw}\ [C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{prwt}+(\delta Z_{q})_{tw}\ [C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{prsw}\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left[c_{1}\ L_{1}+\frac{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}{2M_{1}^{2}}\ L_{2}\ (3c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}})+8|A^{\prime}|^{2}\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\ L_{2}\right]\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{ps})^{\dagger}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{tr})}{M_{1}^{2}}\;.

(3.182)

Schematically for the operator above we have,

\displaystyle\frac{d[C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]_{prst}}{d\ln\mu}\propto\frac{d[C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]_{prst}^{(0)}}{d\ln\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{d\ln\mu}

\displaystyle\left\{(\delta Z_{\ell})_{pw}\ [C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{wrst}+(\delta Z_{\ell})_{rw}\ [C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{pwst}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(\delta Z_{q})_{sw}\ [C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{prwt}+(\delta Z_{q})_{tw}\ [C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{prsw}\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left[2c_{1}+\frac{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\ (3c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}})+16|A^{\prime}|^{2}\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\right]\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{ps})^{\dagger}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{tr})}{M_{1}^{2}}\;.

(3.183)

On the other hand the derivative of the tree-level operator reads,

\displaystyle\frac{d[C^{(1)}_{\ell q}]^{(0)}_{prst}}{d\ln\mu}=\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{ps})^{\dagger}}{4M_{1}^{2}}\ (\beta_{\lambda^{1\text{L}}})_{tr}+\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}})_{tr}}{4M_{1}^{2}}\ (\beta_{(\lambda^{1\text{L}})^{\dagger}})_{ps}-\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{ps})^{\dagger}(\lambda^{1\text{L}})_{tr}}{4M_{1}^{2}}\ \gamma_{M_{1}^{2}}\;,

(3.184)

where
\gamma_{M_{1}^{2}}=d\ln M_{1}^{2}/d\ln\mu
is the anomalous dimension of the leptoquark mass. The last term in (
3.184
) will cancel with the last line of (
3.183
). In contrast to the coupling, this cancellation is not captured by the matching procedure, where we have assumed no heavy-external-field legs.

The remaining terms inside the curly brackets in (
3.183
), due to the redefintion of the fermion fields, exactly cancel the contributions to the
\beta
-function from the tree-level result (
3.184
). Cancellations aside, by taking the explicit derivative of the whole set of Wcs we can cross-check the logarithmic part of our results. Comparing the
\beta
-functions produced by our model, with the relevant parts of
Jenkins:2013zja
;
Jenkins:2013wua
;
Alonso:2013hga
;
Celis:2017hod
, we find complete agreement.

3.5
Phenomenological Aspects

In this section we present some interesting phenomenological aspects
arising from the
S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2}
LQ-model whose one-loop effective Lagrangian
derived previously in this section.

3.5.1
Lepton magnetic and electric dipole moments

There is a recent excitement about the muon anomalous magnetic moment.
FNAL experiment
Abi:2021gix
confirmed previous results by
BNL experiment
Bennett:2006fi

and found a 4.2
\sigma
excess w.r.t the SM,

\Delta a_{\mu}=a_{\mu}^{\rm exp}-a_{\mu}^{\rm SM}=(251\pm 59)\times 10^{-11}

Abi:2021gix
.
As a demonstration of our one-loop effective Lagrangian
we work out the contribution to
\Delta a_{\mu}
from the decoupling of

S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2}
-leptoquarks, and compare it with the fixed order one-loop calculation.

Within functional approach, contributions to magnetic moments of fermions arise from eqs. (
2.58
) and (
2.59
). The corresponding functional supertrace diagram and its expression is displayed in (
2.31
). The above contributions along with the insertion of the tree-level operator
[\mathcal{O}^{(3)}_{\ell equ}]
, associated with
[C^{(3)}_{\ell equ}]
given in eq. (
3.14
), in a one-loop diagram computed for example in ref.
Aebischer:2021uvt
constitute the full EFT formula in this model.
The dominant new physics contributions to the anomalous magnetic moment of the
\ell
-generation lepton are thus

\Delta a^{\ell}\ =\ \frac{4m_{\ell}v}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\frac{1}{g^{\prime}}\Re e[\mathcal{C}_{eB}]\ -\ \frac{1}{g}\Re e[\mathcal{C}_{eW}]\right]_{\ell\ell}+\frac{2N_{c}}{3\pi^{2}}\,\sum_{q}m_{\ell}m_{q}\,\log\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{m_{q}^{2}}\right)\,\Re e\left\{[\mathcal{C}^{(3)}_{\ell equ}]_{\ell\ell qq}\right\}\;,

(3.185)

where
v
is the vev,
m_{\ell}
is the
\ell=e,\mu,\tau
lepton mass and
m_{q}
is the quark mass running in the loop with
q=t,c,u
. We note for later, that when evolving down to the top quark mass,
m_{t}
, the dominant part of the last term vanishes, hence we can neglect all other sub-leading terms in the sum. Therefore, we are left with the first two terms in the square bracket. The coefficients
\mathcal{C}_{eB}
and
\mathcal{C}_{eW}
are defined at low energies in mass basis of ref.
Dedes:2017zog
. They are related to the Warsaw gauge basis coefficients,
C_{eB}
and
C_{eW}
, through the expressions,

\mathcal{C}_{eB}=U_{e_{L}}^{\dagger}C_{eB}U_{e_{R}}\;,\qquad\mathcal{C}_{eW}=U_{e_{L}}^{\dagger}C_{eW}U_{e_{R}}\;,

(3.186)

where the unitary matrices
U_{e_{L,R}}
diagonalize the lepton mass matrices,
U_{e_{L}}^{\dagger}y_{E}U_{e_{R}}=\hat{y}_{E}=diag(y_{e},y_{\mu},y_{\tau})
.
Since our results are given in Green basis we need a translation from Green to Warsaw basis. This translation is nicely given in ref.
Gherardi:2020det
. After a little
algebra, we find the coefficients at renormalization scale
\mu
(still
gauge basis in
3\times 3
matrix notation) to be

\displaystyle[C_{eB}]^{(1)}(\mu)\

\displaystyle=\ \frac{g^{\prime}N_{c}}{16\pi^{2}}\left\{\frac{5}{24}\left[\log\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\right)+\frac{19}{10}\right]\frac{Y_{1U}^{1L}}{M_{1}^{2}}-\frac{1}{24}\frac{y_{E}\cdot\Lambda_{e}}{M_{1}^{2}}-\frac{1}{48}\frac{\tilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}\cdot y_{E}}{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}\right\}\;,

(3.187)

\displaystyle[C_{eW}]^{(1)}(\mu)\

\displaystyle=\ \frac{gN_{c}}{16\pi^{2}}\left\{-\frac{1}{8}\left[\log\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\right)+\frac{3}{2}\right]\frac{Y_{1U}^{1L}}{M_{1}^{2}}+\frac{1}{24}\frac{\Lambda_{\ell}\cdot y_{E}}{M_{1}^{2}}-\frac{1}{48}\frac{\tilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}\cdot y_{E}}{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}\right\}\;,

(3.188)

where
``\cdot"
means matrix multiplication. These results are in agreement with ref.
Aebischer:2021uvt
.
The parameters
Y_{1U}^{1L},\Lambda_{e},\tilde{\Lambda}_{\ell}
and
{\Lambda}_{\ell}
are defined in eqs. (
3.31
),(
3.29
) and (
3.28
), respectively.
We then use the RGE running of the coefficients
C_{eB},C_{eW}

from the heavy leptoquark mass scale
M_{1}
down to the top-quark mass scale
m_{t}

and plug the result into (
3.185
) to find at leading-log approximation (for
N_{c}=3
):
6
6

6

To leading-log order the result is the same by setting
\mu=m_{t}

in (
3.187
) and (
3.188
) and then take the difference in (
3.185
), and neglecting the contribution from light quark masses.

\displaystyle\Delta a^{(S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2})}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=u,c,t}\frac{m_{\ell}}{4\pi^{2}}\>\frac{m_{q}}{M_{1}^{2}}\>\left[\log\left(\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\right)+\frac{7}{4}\right]\Re e(\hat{\lambda}^{1L*}_{q\ell}\hat{\lambda}^{1R}_{q\ell})

\displaystyle-\frac{m_{\ell}^{2}}{32\pi^{2}M_{1}^{2}}\>\left(\hat{\lambda}^{1L*}_{q\ell}\hat{\lambda}^{1L}_{q\ell}+\hat{\lambda}^{1R*}_{q\ell}\hat{\lambda}^{1R}_{q\ell}\right)\;,

(3.189)

where all parameters and masses are to be evaluated at
m_{t}
and the “hatted” couplings are defined in mass basis as,

\hat{\lambda}^{1L}=U_{u_{L}}^{T}{\lambda}^{1L}U_{e_{L}}\;,\quad\hat{\lambda}^{1R}=U_{u_{R}}^{T}{\lambda}^{1R}U_{e_{R}}\;,\quad\hat{\tilde{\lambda}}=U^{\dagger}_{d_{R}}\tilde{\lambda}\,U_{eL}\;,

(3.190)

with
U_{u_{L}}^{\dagger}y_{U}U_{u_{R}}=\hat{y}_{U}=\mathrm{diag}(y_{u},y_{s},y_{t})
being the up-quark fermion Yukawa couplings.
Note that, as it should, the one-loop expression (
3.189
) agrees with the fixed order
calculation of ref.
Bauer:2015knc
for the
S_{1}
-leptoquark decoupling.
Also obvious from (
3.189
) is a natural enhancement of
O(m_{t}/m_{\ell})

due to
S_{1}
-decoupling, while there is no effect from the
\tilde{S}_{2}
-particle decoupling.
However, a similar enhancement is shown in
eq. (
3.49
), for the one-loop corrections to the
Yukawa coupling of the leptons, and subsequently to the lepton mass itself.

Moreover, a bound on electron Electric Dipole Moment (eEDM),
|d_{e}|<1.1\times 10^{-29}~\mathrm{e\cdot cm}
at 90% CL, anounced by ACME collaboration
ACME:2018yjb
almost three years ago.
Our complete 1-loop functional matching LQs renders the calculation of eEDM
very easy. As mentioned previously, in the model at hand (
S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2}
), bosonic operators
of the form
H^{2}F\tilde{F}
and
F^{3}
are not induced, therefore the only effect at one-loop
arises from the Warsaw-basis operators
Q_{eB}
,
Q_{eW}
and
Q^{(3)}_{\ell equ}
as before. Basically, the calculation is the same with the lepton magnetic moments we performed above. Again, neglecting the contribution from
C^{(3)}_{\ell equ}
since the evaluation is at
\mu=m_{t}
, the eEDM reads,

\frac{d_{e}}{e}(m_{t})\ =\ \sqrt{2}\;v\left[\frac{1}{g^{\prime}}\Im m[\mathcal{C}_{eB}(m_{t})]\ -\ \frac{1}{g}\Im m[\mathcal{C}_{eW}(m_{t})]\right]_{11}\;.

(3.191)

Plugging into (
3.191
) the imaginary parts of eqs. (
3.187
) and (
3.188
) after substituting
\mu=m_{t}
,
we find

\displaystyle\frac{d_{e}}{e}\ =\ \frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\,\frac{m_{t}}{M_{1}^{2}}\,\left[\log\left(\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}\right)+\frac{7}{4}\right]\Im m(\hat{\lambda}^{1L\,*}_{31}\hat{\lambda}^{1R}_{31})\;.

(3.192)

This result agrees with the fixed order 1-loop calculation, up to
\mathcal{O}(m_{t}^{2}/M_{1}^{4})
-terms,
obtained by applying the general one-loop formula for lepton EDMs
from ref.
Dedes:2007ef
onto the particular
S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2}
LQ-model. The leading-log
term of (
3.192
) also agrees with the one obtained in refs.
Dorsner:2016wpm
;
Panico:2018hal
.

The reader should note that this is a SMEFT calculation, i.e. we have chosen
\mu=m_{t}
. Ideally it’s more appropriate to match onto Low Energy Effective Field Theory (LEFT) and perform the calculation of dipole moments at lower scales, which is beyond the scope of this paper. For a thorough EFT analysis of leptonic magnetic and electric dipole moments along these lines the reader is referred to refs.
Dekens:2018bci
;
Aebischer:2021uvt
.

3.5.2
Radiative Neutrino masses

A nice feature of the
S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2}
model is that neutrino masses are induced radiatively at one-loop. The single dimension-5 operator
Q_{\nu\nu}
, defined in Appendix
C
,
arises in the effective Lagrangian from the supertrace functional diagram (
2.34
) which after calculation provides us the associated Wilson coefficient
G_{\nu\nu}=C_{\nu\nu}

(
3.54
) in both Green and Warsaw basis. The result is finite and agrees with ref.
Crivellin:2020mjs
.
Then going to the mass basis SMEFT Lagrangian of ref.
Dedes:2017zog
we have for the
diagonal neutrino mass matrix

m_{\nu}\ =\ -v^{2}\>U_{\nu_{L}}^{T}\,C_{\nu\nu}\,U_{\nu_{L}}\;.

(3.193)

By using the
tree level definitions of CKM and MNS matrices
7
7

7

There is no ”pollution” to CKM or MNS matrices from other tree level operators in this model.
,
as
K_{\rm CKM}=U_{u_{L}}^{\dagger}U_{d_{L}}
and
U_{\rm MNS}=U_{e_{L}}^{\dagger}U_{\nu_{L}}
and eq. (
3.190
) we obtain

m_{\nu}\ =\ -\frac{\sqrt{2}}{16\pi^{2}}\frac{vA_{\tilde{2}1}}{M_{1}^{2}-\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}\>\left[U_{\rm MNS}^{T}\,(\hat{\lambda}^{1L})^{T}\,K_{\rm CKM}\,m_{d}\hat{\tilde{\lambda}}\,U_{\rm MNS}\right]\log\left(\frac{M_{1}^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}\right)\;,

(3.194)

where
m_{d}
is the diagonal down quark mass matrix. The neutrino masses clearly follow a “down-quark” mass hierarchy with
the couplings
\hat{\lambda}^{1L}
,
\hat{\tilde{\lambda}}
and the CKM matrix defining off-diagonal transitions. Obviously, depending
on the value of the parameter
A_{\tilde{2}1}
that mixes both leptoquarks with the Higgs boson, we can probe two different mass
scales: (i)
A_{\tilde{2}1}/max(M_{1},M_{2})\simeq 1
, and (ii)
A_{\tilde{2}1}/max(M_{1},M_{2})\to 0
. In case (i) correct order of magnitude of neutrino
mass,
m_{\nu}\lesssim 0.1
eV, and
\lambda\sim O(1)
requires
M_{1}\approx M_{2}\gtrsim 10^{13}
GeV and this is currently consistent with proton decay bounds (see below),
whereas in case (ii)
M_{1}\approx M_{2}\simeq 1
TeV requires
A_{\tilde{2}1}\approx 10^{-7}
GeV which is technically natural in terms of a
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
(or
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
)
softly broken discrete symmetry discussed in section
3
(but still baryon number violating couplings,
\lambda^{\not{B}L}
and
\lambda^{\not{B}R}
, are allowed and have to be set to zero by another symmetry in
order to avoid fast proton decay).

3.5.3
Proton decay

Baryon number is violated in the
S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2}
model.
All

d=6

baryon number
violating (BNV) operators appear already at tree level in the effective Lagrangian [eqs. (
3.19
),(
3.20
)],
after the decoupling of
S_{1}
-field. From these expressions and Table
3

we easily see they have
\Delta B=\Delta L=1
consistent with eqs. (
3.24
)
and (
3.25
). Rotating fermion fields into the mass basis of
ref.
Dedes:2017zog

we find for the tree-level Wilson-coefficients

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{C}^{qqu}\right]^{(0)}_{f_{1}f_{2}f_{3}f_{4}}\

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\>[K_{\rm CKM}^{T}\,(\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}L}})^{*}]_{f_{1}f_{2}}\,(\hat{\lambda}^{1R})_{f_{3}f_{4}}\;,

(3.195)

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{C}^{duq}\right]^{(0)}_{f_{1}f_{2}f_{3}f_{4}}\

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\>(\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}R}})^{*}_{f_{1}f_{2}}\,[K_{\rm CKM}^{T}\,\hat{\lambda}^{1L}]_{f_{3}f_{4}}\;,

(3.196)

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{C}^{duu}\right]^{(0)}_{f_{1}f_{2}f_{3}f_{4}}\

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{M_{1}^{2}}\>(\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}R}})^{*}_{f_{1}f_{2}}\>(\hat{\lambda}^{1R})_{f_{3}f_{4}}\;,

(3.197)

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{C}^{qqq}\right]^{(0)}_{f_{1}f_{2}f_{3}f_{4}}\

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\frac{2}{M_{1}^{2}}\>[K_{\rm CKM}^{T}\>(\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}L}})^{*}]_{f_{1}f_{2}}\>[K^{T}_{\rm CKM}\hat{\lambda}^{1L}]_{f_{3}f_{4}}\;,

(3.198)

where the “hatted” couplings are given in eq. (
3.190
) in addition to

\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}L}}\ =\ U^{\dagger}_{u_{L}}\lambda^{\not{B}L}U_{d_{L}}^{*}\;,\qquad\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}R}}\ =\ U^{\dagger}_{d_{R}}\lambda^{\not{B}R}U_{u_{R}}^{*}\;.

(3.199)

Obviously, due to lepton and baryon quantum numbers
arranged in Table
3
, only one BNV-coupling,
\lambda^{\not{B}L}
or
\lambda^{\not{B}R}
, appear for
\Delta L=\Delta B=1
.
Plugging these into the Feynman Rules of ref.
Dedes:2017zog
we derive
decay rates for general nucleon decay processes.

To date, proton decay has not been observed and Super Kamiokande has
increased the proton lifetime limits up to
\sim 10^{34}
years with bounds
Super-Kamiokande:2016exg
;
Super-Kamiokande:2014otb

\tau(p\rightarrow e^{+}\pi^{0})>1.6\times 10^{34}~\mathrm{yrs}\;,\qquad\tau(p\rightarrow\bar{\nu}K^{+})>0.59\times 10^{34}~\mathrm{yrs}\;,

(3.200)

being the most sensitive ones to BSM physics
Nath:2006ut
.
Based on the first of these bounds
we derive constraints on the following products of couplings:

2(\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}R}})^{*}_{11}\hat{\lambda}_{11}^{1R}\;,\quad 2(\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}R}})^{*}_{11}\hat{\lambda}_{11}^{1L}\;,\quad 2(\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}L}})^{*}_{11}\hat{\lambda}_{11}^{1R}\;,\quad 4(\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}L}})^{*}_{11}\hat{\lambda}_{11}^{1L}\ \lesssim\ 10^{-6}\,\left(\frac{M_{1}}{3\times 10^{12}}\right)^{2}\;.

(3.201)

Note that although the CKM-matrix,
K_{\rm CKM}
,
appears explicitly in tree expressions (
3.195
)-(
3.198
), it disappears completely from
the relevant BNV vertices of ref.
Dedes:2017zog
for physical fields.
In summary,
M_{1}\gtrsim 3\times 10^{12}~{\rm GeV}
and first generation

\hat{\lambda}^{1L,1R}\sim O(1)
and
\lambda^{\not{B}L}
and
\lambda^{\not{B}R}
of the order of electron yukawa coupling is, currently, a safe combination.

At tree level, the proton decay constraints in eq. (
3.201
)
apply on the 11 entries of
\lambda
-matrices. This changes by going to higher orders where in principle all flavour structure of those matrices are getting involved through the
CKM-matrix. One loop contributions to BNV Wilson coefficients are given in eqs. (
3.3.5
)-(
3.168
).
For example, by just looking at
[G_{qqq}]^{(1)}
and the term proportional to
the strong QCD coupling,
g_{s}^{2}(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{ps})^{*}\,(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{st})
we find a contribution
to the
p\to e^{+}\pi^{0}
amplitude of the form
g_{s}^{2}(\hat{\lambda^{\not{B}L}}K_{\rm CKM}^{\dagger})_{11}\,(K_{CKM}^{T}\hat{\lambda}^{1L})_{11}
which, although CKM-suppressed, displays a certain sensitivity in the off-diagonal
\lambda_{1i}
entries under the strong proton decay bounds of (
3.200
). A partial list of
dedicated studies on proton decay in leptoquark-like
models are given in refs.
Dorsner:2012nq
;
Baldes:2011mh
;
Hambye:2017qix
;
Helo:2019yqp
and related reviews in refs.
Nath:2006ut
;
Heeck:2019kgr
.

In summary, unless there is a symmetry to prohibit BNV-couplings, the mass
M_{1}

must be bigger than the “intermediate” scale
\sim 10^{12}
GeV.
As we discuss in the next paragraph,
this will bring every other leptoquark masses e.g.
\tilde{M}_{2}
, at around that scale
unless unnatural fine tuning is called upon in the Higgs sector.

3.5.4
Perturbativity and Fine Tuning

An interesting interplay between the LQ masses can be explored in some operators that contain the ratio of the two masses. We can thus put some bounds on the ratio
M_{1}/\tilde{M}_{2}
so that perturbation theory is not violated. For example, for the operator
G_{\ell d}
adding the tree, eq.(
3.15
), and one-loop level, eq.(
3.3.5
) that depends on the LQ mass ratio, we have,

\displaystyle\left[G_{\ell d}\right]_{prst}\propto-\frac{\tilde{\lambda}^{\ast}_{tp}\tilde{\lambda}_{sr}}{2\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}\,\left(1+\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\,\frac{M_{1}^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}(N_{c}c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}})(1+L_{1})\right)\;.

(3.202)

Other operators depend on the inverse mass ratio
\tilde{M}_{2}/M_{1}
. For instance, adding eq.(
3.16
) and eq.(
3.3.5
), we have,

\displaystyle\left[G_{\ell q}^{(1)}\right]_{prst}\propto\frac{(\lambda^{1\text{L}})_{sp}^{\ast}(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{tr})}{4M_{1}^{2}}\,\left(1+\frac{2}{16\pi^{2}}\,\frac{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}{M_{1}^{2}}(N_{c}\,c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}})(1+L_{2})\right)\;.

(3.203)

For perturbation theory to work, loop level contributions have to be way smaller than tree level ones. Then we can get a combined constrain for the ratio of the masses,

\displaystyle 2\frac{N_{c}c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}}}{16\pi^{2}}\,(1+L_{2})<\frac{M_{1}^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}<\frac{16\pi^{2}}{(1+L_{1})(N_{c}\,c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}})}\;,

(3.204)

which depends explicitly on parameters of the self interactions of the leptoquarks. Next, we define the following quantities,

\displaystyle r^{2}\equiv\frac{M_{1}^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}\,,\qquad\qquad\alpha\equiv\frac{N_{c}\,c^{(1)}_{1\tilde{2}}+c^{(2)}_{1\tilde{2}}}{16\pi^{2}}\;.

(3.205)

The inequality then becomes,

\displaystyle 2\alpha(1+L_{2})<r^{2}<\frac{1}{\alpha(1+L_{1})}\;.

(3.206)

Which can be written in the following form,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{2}}{\tilde{M}_{2}^{2}}\,\exp\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha r^{2}}\right)<r^{2}<\frac{M_{1}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\,\exp\left(\frac{r^{2}}{2\alpha}-1\right)\;.

(3.207)

From here on we will pick up a renormalization scale, first we will pick
\mu=M_{1}
and then
\mu=\tilde{M}_{2}
. As for the parameters
c_{1\tilde{2}}^{(1,2)}
we will take them first of order unity and second to a fine tuned value of the order of
10^{-4}
. Plugging these numbers into eq.(
3.205
) we get
\alpha=1/4\pi^{2}
and
\alpha=10^{-4}/4\pi^{2}
for each respective value of c’s. Both these values will be used for both cases of the renormalization scale and extract some bounds for the masses.

•

For the first case,
\mu=M_{1}
. In this case the combined inequality reads, for
N_{c}=3
,

\displaystyle r^{2}\,\exp\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha r^{2}}\right)<r^{2}<\exp\left(\frac{r^{2}}{2\alpha}-1\right)\;.

(3.208)

The right inequality is always satisfied, while from the left one we can get that,

\displaystyle r^{2}<\frac{1}{\alpha}\;.

(3.209)

Considering the two distinct values of
\alpha
, we can get
M_{1}<(2\pi,\,2\pi\times 10^{2})\,\tilde{M}_{2}
.

•

For the second case
\mu=\tilde{M}_{2}
. The inequality becomes,

\displaystyle\exp\left(1-\frac{1}{\alpha r^{2}}\right)<r^{2}<r^{2}\,\exp\left(\frac{r^{2}}{2\alpha}-1\right)\;.

(3.210)

The right part gives,
r^{2}>2\alpha
, while the left part is always true.

If we combine the two results we can get the following bound,

\displaystyle 2\alpha<r^{2}<\frac{1}{\alpha}\;.

(3.211)

Plugging the values of
\alpha
we get,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\,(1,\,10^{-2})<\frac{M_{1}}{\tilde{M}_{2}}<2\pi\,(1,\,10^{2})\;.

(3.212)

Therefore, we can conclude that when couplings
c_{1\tilde{2}}^{(1,2)}
are of
\mathcal{O}(1)
the masses can be taken to be of similar magnitude without violating perturbation theory. If we fine tune the couplings to even smaller values we can further increase the mass ratio giving us more room to tune the numerical values of the masses.

Noteworthy, the situation we just described for the Wcs,
G_{\ell d}^{(1)}
and
G_{\ell q}^{(1)}
,
share the same characteristics with
the Higgs mass hierarchy problem which is of course evident in LQ-models.
Indeed, by performing the matching procedure we have assumed that the Higgs mass
m
is zero i.e.,
the Higgs field is part of the low energy EFT. Therefore,
one-loop contributions to the Higgs mass found in (
3.53
)
have to be of the order of the EW scale. For this to happen there are two cases
(i)

LQ-masses are of the order of the TeV-scale and Higgs couplings naturally
of order
\mathcal{O}(1)
or
(ii)

LQ-masses are heavier but Higgs couplings e.g.
\lambda_{H1},\tilde{\lambda}_{H2},\lambda_{\tilde{2}\tilde{2}}
together with
A_{\tilde{2}1}/M_{1}
are small enough, although there is
no known symmetry to naturally accommodate all these limits.

4
Conclusions

The resurgence of functional techniques to matching has led to a fair amount of universal results and compact formulae over the last few years. In this work we explored the matching of all scalar leptoquark representations that can be constructed under the SM gauge group. We have extracted through the use of
Supertrace functional techniques

Cohen:2020fcu
a universal formula, eq. (
2.9
) for tree level and eq. (
2.43
) for one-loop matching plus all

\mathbf{X}
-matrices in Appendix
A
, for the decoupling of all scalar leptoquarks and put it to use in two distinct models. First we cross-tested it with the Feynman diagrammatic approach of the
S_{1}+S_{3}
model
Gherardi:2020det
. Then we applied it to the, phenomenologically richer,
S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2}
model taking also baryon number violating couplings into account. In total, the latter model generates the single dimension-5 Weinberg operator at one loop, which gives rise to radiative neutrino masses. At dimension-6, 109 operators are generated in the Green basis while in the translation to Warsaw basis we are left with 53 (out of 59) operators thus covering almost the entire spectrum of dimension-6 operators, the only exception being the set of 6 bosonic CP-violating ones. Our results of the given Wilson coefficients, derived in section
3
,
have been also cross checked with the one loop RGEs finding complete agreement.

On the phenomenological side, we have briefly explored several distinct observables. First, we studied the implications to the lepton magnetic and electric dipole moments where there has been recent experimental advances. With this example we demonstrated the use of the matching
in arriving at know results from fixed order calculations.
Secondly, we have investigated possible regions of leptoquark masses, at the scale of a few TeV and at a high scale, as well as their coupling with the Higgs field, to generate radiatively the order of magnitude of neutrino masses through the Weinberg operator. Furthermore, we were able to put certain bounds in the combinations of BNV and non-BNV couplings, through the investigation of proton decay at tree level and one-loop. Last but not least, we have constructed a combined inequality for the ratio of the two LQ masses so that perturbation theory is not violated and briefly discussed the hierarchy problem in LQ-models.

As a concluding remark we would like to point out that models covering almost the entirety of the given operator basis spectrum, can serve as excellent
benchmarks
for various codes that will perform the matching automatically. The main reason for this argument is that within matching one needs to apply a fair amount of identities ranging from group theoretic, to Fierz identities and also accounting for evanescent operators arising from higher number of
\gamma
-matrix structures. All of the above have been encountered in the models that have been investigated in this work, ultimately, adding up to the number of fully worked out examples of one loop matching.

Appendix A
Lagrangian and
\mathbf{X}
-matrices for scalar leptoquarks

We append here the general Lagrangian for all scalar leptoquarks and the relevant
\mathbf{X}
-matrices needed to construct the EFT Lagrangian at one-loop.
It can be split into three parts, in eqs. (
2.1
),(
2.2
) and (
2.3
). The notation for LQs is given in Table
1
.

The LQ-SM fermion interactions are,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{LQ-f}}

\displaystyle=\left[\left(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr}\right)\bar{q}^{c}_{pi}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\ell_{r}+\left(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{pr}\right)\bar{u}_{i}^{c}\,e_{r}\right]S_{1i}+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle+\left[(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})\,\epsilon^{ijk}\,\bar{q}_{pj}\cdot\epsilon\cdot q^{c}_{rk}+(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})\,\epsilon^{ijk}\bar{d}_{pj}\,u^{c}_{rk}\right]S_{1i}+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle+\left[(\tilde{\lambda}^{1}_{pr})\,\bar{d}^{c}_{pi}\,e_{r}+(\tilde{\lambda}^{1\not{B}}_{pr})\,\epsilon^{ijk}\,\bar{u}_{pj}\,u^{c}_{rk}\right]\,\tilde{S}_{1i}+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle+\left[(\lambda^{2LR}_{pr})\,\bar{q}_{pi\alpha}e_{r}-(\lambda^{2RL}_{pr})\,\bar{u}_{pi}\,\ell_{r\beta}\,\epsilon^{\beta\alpha}\right]\,S_{2i\alpha}+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle+(\tilde{\lambda}_{pr})\,\bar{d}_{pi}\tilde{S}^{T}_{2i}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\ell_{r}+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle+\left[(\lambda^{3L}_{pr})\,\bar{q}^{c}_{pi}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot\ell_{r}+(\lambda^{3\not{B}}_{pr})\,\epsilon^{ijk}\,\bar{q}_{pj}\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot\epsilon\cdot q^{c}_{rk}\right]S_{3i}^{I}+\text{h.c.}\;.

(A.1)

The LQ-Higgs interactions read,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{LQ-H}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n}\,\left(M_{n}^{2}+\lambda_{Hn}\;|S_{n}|^{2}\right)\,|H|^{2}+\sum_{n=2,\tilde{2}}\,\lambda_{nn}\,(S^{\dagger}_{ni}\cdot H)\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot S_{ni})

\displaystyle\left[-A_{\tilde{2}1}\,S^{\dagger}_{1i}\,(\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{2i}\cdot H)+A_{\tilde{2}3}\,S^{I\dagger}_{3i}\,(\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{2i}\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot H)\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}\,(S^{\dagger}_{2i}\cdot H)\,(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\tilde{S}_{2i})+\lambda_{3\tilde{1}}\,\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{1i}\,(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot H)S^{I}_{3i}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\lambda_{H13}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot H)\,S^{I\dagger}_{3i}\,S_{1i}+\text{h.c.}\right]

\displaystyle-i\lambda_{\epsilon H3}\,\epsilon^{IJK}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot H)S^{J\dagger}_{3i}\,S^{K\dagger}_{3i}\;.

(A.2)

Where the index
n
runs through,
n=1,\tilde{1},2,\tilde{2},3
in one to one correspondence to the sets
\left\{S_{1},\tilde{S}_{1},S_{2},\tilde{S}_{2},S_{3}\right\}
,
\left\{M_{1},\tilde{M}_{1},M_{2},\tilde{M}_{2},M_{3}\right\}
and
\left\{\lambda_{H1},\tilde{\lambda}_{H1},\lambda_{H2},\tilde{\lambda}_{H2},\lambda_{H3}\right\}
.

Finally, self-interactions among scalar leptoquarks are

\mathcal{L}_{\rm S}\ =\ -V(S)\;,

(A.3)

where
V(S)
is the tree-level potential that is built from gauge group invariant combinations among LQ fields.
In general
V(S)
for all LQs is quite lengthy. For combinations
S_{1}+\tilde{S}_{2}
, the potential
V(S_{1},\tilde{S}_{2})
can be
read from (
3.4
), for
S_{1}+S_{3}
from eq. (2.3) of ref.
Gherardi:2020det
while the most general one
in eqs. (46) and (49) of ref.
Crivellin:2021tmz
. Note that
X(U)
-matrices are constructed solely from simple second field
derivatives of
V(S)
and there is no need to be written down explicitly.

In what follows we reserve letters
i,\alpha,p,\mu,A
and
I
to denote the respective field indices in the
left
hand side multiplet
\bar{\varphi}=\left(\bar{\varphi}_{S},\,\bar{\varphi}_{L}\right)
, while the letters
j,\beta,r,\nu,B,J
are used for the
right
hand side multiplet
\varphi=\left(\varphi_{S},\,\varphi_{L}\right)
and we suppress spinor indices. Each letter represents the respective gauge group representation given in Table
1
. Additionally, the chirality projection operators regarding the Weyl to Dirac conversion of fermions mentioned in the main text, are left implicit.

A.1
\mathbf{X_{SS}}

\underline{\mathbf{U_{S_{1}S_{1}}}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}S_{1}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H1}|H|^{2}\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{\dagger}_{1i}\,\partial S_{1j}}\;,

(A.4)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}S_{1}^{\dagger}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H1}|H|^{2}\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{1i}\,\partial S^{\dagger}_{1j}}\;.

(A.5)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{S_{1}\tilde{S}_{2}}}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}\tilde{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle=A_{\tilde{2}1}^{\ast}H_{\beta}^{\ast}\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{1i}\,\partial\tilde{S}_{2j\beta}}\;,

(A.6)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}\tilde{S}_{2}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=A_{\tilde{2}1}H_{\beta}\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{\dagger}_{1i}\,\partial\tilde{S}^{\ast}_{2j\beta}}\;.

(A.7)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{S_{1}S_{3}}}}

\displaystyle U_{S^{\dagger}_{1}S_{3}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H13}^{\ast}\,\delta_{ij}\,(H^{\dagger}\sigma^{J}H)+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{\dagger}_{1i}\,\partial S^{J}_{3j}}\;,

(A.8)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}S^{\ast}_{3}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H13}\,\delta_{ij}\,(H^{\dagger}\sigma^{J}H)+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{1i}\,\partial S^{I\ast}_{3j}}\;.

(A.9)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{1}\tilde{S}_{1}}}}

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}\tilde{S}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\lambda}_{H1}|H|^{2}\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{1i}\,\partial\tilde{S}_{1j}}\;,

(A.10)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\lambda}_{H1}|H|^{2}\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}_{1i}\,\partial\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{1j}}\;.

(A.11)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{1}S_{3}}}}

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}S_{3}}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{3\tilde{1}}\,\delta_{ij}\,(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\sigma^{J}\cdot H)+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{1i}\,\partial S^{J}_{3j}}\;,

(A.12)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}S_{3}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{3\tilde{1}}^{\ast}\,\delta_{ij}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{J}\cdot\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}_{1i}\,\partial S^{J\ast}_{3j}}\;.

(A.13)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{S_{2}S_{2}}}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}S_{2}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ij}\delta_{\alpha\beta}\lambda_{H2}|H|^{2}-\lambda_{22}\,\delta_{ij}\,H_{\alpha}\,H^{\ast}_{\beta}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{\dagger}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial S_{2j\beta}}\;,

(A.14)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{T}S_{2}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ij}\delta_{\alpha\beta}\lambda_{H2}|H|^{2}-\lambda_{22}\,\delta_{ij}\,H^{\ast}_{\alpha}\,H_{\beta}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{T}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial S^{\ast}_{2j\beta}}\;.

(A.15)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{S_{2}\tilde{S}_{2}}}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}\tilde{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}\,\delta_{ij}\,H_{\alpha}\,(\epsilon\cdot H)^{T}_{\beta}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{\dagger}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial\tilde{S}_{2j\beta}}\;,

(A.16)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{T}\tilde{S}_{2}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}\,\delta_{ij}\,H^{\ast}_{\alpha}\,(\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})_{\beta}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S^{T}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial\tilde{S}^{\ast}_{2j\beta}}\;.

(A.17)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{2}\tilde{S}_{2}}}}

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}\tilde{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ij}\delta_{\alpha\beta}\tilde{\lambda}_{H2}|H|^{2}-\lambda_{\tilde{2}\tilde{2}}\,\delta_{ij}\,H_{\alpha}\,H^{\ast}_{\beta}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial\tilde{S}_{2j\beta}}\;,

(A.18)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}\tilde{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{3}\lambda_{5}\epsilon^{ijk}\left[\epsilon^{\alpha\alpha_{1}}\tilde{S}_{2j\alpha_{1}}H_{\beta}^{\ast}+\tilde{S}_{2k\alpha_{1}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\beta}H_{\alpha}^{\ast}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}(H^{\dagger}\cdot\tilde{S}_{2k})\right]+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}^{T}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial\tilde{S}_{2j\beta}}\;,

(A.19)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}\tilde{S}_{2}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{3}\lambda_{5}\epsilon^{ijk}\left[\epsilon^{\alpha\alpha_{1}}\tilde{S}_{2k\alpha_{1}}H_{\beta}+\tilde{S}_{2k\alpha_{1}}\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\beta}H_{\alpha}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}(\tilde{S}_{2k}^{\dagger}\cdot H)\right]+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial\tilde{S}^{\ast}_{2j\beta}}\;,

(A.20)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}\tilde{S}_{2}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ij}\delta_{\alpha\beta}\tilde{\lambda}_{H2}|H|^{2}-\lambda_{\tilde{2}\tilde{2}}\,\delta_{ij}\,H^{\ast}_{\alpha}\,H_{\beta}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}^{T}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial\tilde{S}^{\ast}_{2j\beta}}\;.

(A.21)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{2}S_{1}}}}

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}S_{1}}

\displaystyle=A^{\ast}_{\tilde{2}1}H_{\alpha}^{\ast}\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}^{T}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial S_{1j}}\;,

(A.22)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}S_{1}^{\dagger}}

\displaystyle=A_{\tilde{2}1}H_{\alpha}\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial S^{\dagger}_{1j}}\;.

(A.23)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{2}S_{2}}}}

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}S_{2}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}\,\delta_{ij}\,H_{\beta}^{\ast}\,(\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})_{\alpha}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}^{\ast}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial S_{2j\beta}}\;,

(A.24)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}S_{2}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}\,\delta_{ij}\,H_{\beta}\,(\epsilon\cdot H)^{T}_{\alpha}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial S^{\ast}_{2j\beta}}\;.

(A.25)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{2}S_{3}}}}

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}S_{3}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=A_{\tilde{2}3}\,\delta_{ij}\,(\sigma^{J}\cdot H)_{\alpha}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}_{2i\alpha}^{\ast}\,\partial S^{J\ast}_{3j}}\;,

(A.26)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}S_{3}}

\displaystyle=A^{\ast}_{\tilde{2}3}\,\delta_{ij}\,(\sigma^{J}\cdot H^{\ast})^{T}_{\alpha}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial\tilde{S}_{2i\alpha}\,\partial S^{J}_{3j}}\;.

(A.27)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{S_{3}S_{3}}}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}S_{3}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H3}\,\delta_{ij}\,\delta^{IJ}\,|H|^{2}+i\lambda_{\epsilon H3}\,\epsilon^{IJK}\,(H^{\dagger}\sigma^{K}H)\,\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{3i}^{I\ast}\,\partial S^{J}_{3j}}\;,

(A.28)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{T}S_{3}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H3}\,\delta_{ij}\,\delta^{IJ}\,|H|^{2}-i\lambda_{\epsilon H3}\,\epsilon^{IJK}\,(H^{\dagger}\sigma^{K}H)\,\delta_{ij}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{3i}^{I}\,\partial S^{J\ast}_{3j}}\;.

(A.29)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{S_{3}S_{1}}}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}S_{1}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H13}\,\delta_{ij}\,(H^{\dagger}\sigma^{I}H)+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{3i}^{I\ast}\,\partial S_{1j}}\;,

(A.30)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{T}S_{1}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H13}^{\ast}\,\delta_{ij}\,(H^{\dagger}\sigma^{I}H)+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{3i}^{I}\,\partial S^{\ast}_{1j}}\;.

(A.31)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{S_{3}\tilde{S}_{1}}}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}\tilde{S}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{3\tilde{1}}^{\ast}\,\delta_{ij}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{3i}^{I\ast}\,\partial\tilde{S}_{1j}}\;,

(A.32)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{T}\tilde{S}_{1}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{3\tilde{1}}\,\delta_{ij}\,(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot H^{\ast})+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{3i}^{I}\,\partial\tilde{S}^{\ast}_{1j}}\;.

(A.33)

\underline{\mathbf{U_{S_{3}\tilde{S}_{2}}}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}\tilde{S}_{2}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=-A_{\tilde{2}3}\,\delta_{ij}\,(\sigma^{I}\cdot H)_{\beta}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{3i}^{I\ast}\,\partial\tilde{S}^{\ast}_{2j\beta}}\;,

(A.34)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{T}\tilde{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-A_{\tilde{2}3}^{\ast}\,\delta_{ij}\,(\sigma^{I}\cdot H^{\ast})^{T}_{\beta}+\frac{\partial^{2}V}{\partial S_{3i}^{I}\,\partial\tilde{S}_{2j\beta}}\;.

(A.35)

Matrix Structure

\displaystyle\mathbf{U_{S_{n}S_{m}}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{S^{\dagger}_{n}S_{m}}&U_{S^{\dagger}_{n}S^{\ast}_{m}}\\
U_{S^{T}_{n}S_{m}}&U_{S^{T}_{n}S^{\ast}_{m}}\end{pmatrix}\;,

(A.36)

with
n,m=1,\tilde{1},2,\tilde{2},3
. All combinations make up the whole matrix structure of the heavy-only
\mathbf{U_{SS}}
. Here we have listed all terms involving the Higgs field as well. There are also terms coming from the potential of all leptoquarks which are found by the general formula,

\displaystyle U_{S_{n}^{\dagger}S_{m}}=\frac{\partial V}{\partial S_{n}^{\dagger}\,\partial S_{m}}\;,\quad U_{S_{n}^{\dagger}S_{m}^{\ast}}=\frac{\partial V}{\partial S_{n}^{\dagger}\,\partial S_{m}^{\ast}}\;,\quad U_{S_{n}^{T}S_{m}}=\frac{\partial V}{\partial S_{n}^{T}\,\partial S_{m}}\;,\qquad U_{S_{n}^{T}S_{m}^{\ast}}=\frac{\partial V}{\partial S_{n}^{T}\,\partial S_{m}^{\ast}}\;.

(A.37)

A.2
\mathbf{X_{SL}}

\mathbf{U_{S_{n}f}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}\ell}=-(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr})\,\bar{q}^{c}_{pi\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\;,\hfill

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}\ell^{c}}=-(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr})^{\ast}\,\bar{q}_{pi\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\;,

(A.38)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}q}=2(\lambda^{\not{B}L})^{\ast}\bar{q}^{c}_{pk\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\epsilon^{ijk}\;,\hfill

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}q^{c}}=(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr})^{\dagger}\delta_{ij}\bar{\ell}_{p\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\;,

(A.39)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}q}=(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr})^{T}\,\delta_{ij}\,\bar{\ell}^{c}_{p\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\;,\hfill

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}q^{c}}=2(\lambda^{\not{B}L}_{pr})\epsilon^{ijk}\bar{q}_{pk\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\;,

(A.40)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}u}=(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{\ast}\,\bar{d}^{c}_{pk}\,\epsilon^{ijk}\;,\hfill

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}u^{c}}=-(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{pr})^{\dagger}\,\bar{e}_{p}\,\delta_{ij}\;,

(A.41)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}u}=-(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{pr})^{T}\bar{e}^{c}_{p}\,\delta_{ij}\;,\hfill

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}u^{c}}=(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})\epsilon^{ijk}\bar{d}_{pk}\;,

(A.42)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}e}=-(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{pr})\bar{u}^{c}_{pi}\;,\hfill

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}e^{c}}=-(\lambda^{1\text{R}}_{pr})^{\ast}\,\bar{u}_{pi}\;,

(A.43)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}d}=-(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{\dagger}\,\bar{u}^{c}_{pk}\epsilon^{ijk}\;,\hfill

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}d^{c}}=-(\lambda^{\not{B}R}_{pr})^{T}\,\epsilon^{ijk}\bar{u}_{pk}\;,

(A.44)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}u}=-2(\tilde{\lambda}^{1\not{B}}_{pr})^{\dagger}\,\bar{u}^{c}_{pk}\,\epsilon^{ijk}\;,

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}u^{c}}=2(\tilde{\lambda}^{1\not{B}}_{pr})\,\bar{u}_{pk}\,\epsilon^{ijk}\;,

(A.45)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}e}=-(\tilde{\lambda}^{1}_{pr})\,\bar{d}^{c}_{pi}\;,

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}e^{c}}=-(\tilde{\lambda}^{1}_{pr})^{\ast}\,\bar{d}_{pi}\;,

(A.46)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}d}=-(\tilde{\lambda}^{1}_{pr})^{T}\,\bar{e}_{p}^{c}\,\delta_{ij}\;,

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}d^{c}}=-(\tilde{\lambda}^{1}_{pr})^{\dagger}\,\bar{e}_{p}\,\delta_{ij}\;,

(A.47)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{T}\ell}=-(\lambda^{2RL}_{pr})\,\bar{u}_{pi}\,\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\;,

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}\ell^{c}}=-(\lambda^{2RL}_{pr})^{\ast}\,\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\,\bar{u}^{c}_{pi}\;,

(A.48)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}q}=-(\lambda^{2LR}_{pr})^{\dagger}\,\delta_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{ij}\,\bar{e}_{p}\;,

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{T}q^{c}}=-(\lambda^{2LR}_{pr})^{T}\,\delta_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{ij}\,\bar{e}^{c}_{p}\;,

(A.49)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}u}=(\lambda^{2RL}_{pr})^{\dagger}\,\delta_{ij}\,\bar{\ell}_{p\beta}\epsilon^{\beta\alpha}\;,

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{T}u^{c}}=(\lambda^{2RL}_{pr})^{T}\,\delta_{ij}\,\bar{\ell}^{c}_{p\beta}\,\epsilon^{\beta\alpha}\;,

(A.50)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{T}e}=-(\lambda^{2LR}_{pr})\,\bar{q}_{pi\alpha}\;,

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}e^{c}}=-(\lambda^{2LR}_{pr})^{\ast}\,\bar{q}^{c}_{pi\alpha}\;,

(A.51)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}\ell}=-(\tilde{\lambda}_{pr})\,\bar{d}_{pi}\,\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\;,\hfill

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}\ell^{c}}=-(\tilde{\lambda}_{pr})^{\ast}\,\bar{d}^{c}_{pi}\,\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\;,

(A.52)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}d}=(\tilde{\lambda}_{pr})^{\dagger}\,\delta_{ij}\bar{\ell}_{p\beta}\,\epsilon^{\beta\alpha}\;,\hfill

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}d^{c}}=(\tilde{\lambda}_{pr})^{T}\delta_{ij}\,\bar{\ell}^{c}_{p\beta}\,\epsilon^{\beta\alpha}\;,

(A.53)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{T}\ell}=-(\lambda^{3L}_{pr})\,\bar{q}^{c}_{pi\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha\gamma}\,\sigma^{I}_{\gamma\beta}\;,

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}\ell^{c}}=(\lambda^{3L}_{pr})^{\ast}\,\bar{q}_{pi\alpha}\epsilon^{\gamma\alpha}\,\sigma^{I}_{\beta\gamma}\;,

(A.54)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{T}q}=-(\lambda^{3L}_{pr})^{T}\,\bar{\ell}^{c}_{p\alpha}\,\sigma^{I}_{\gamma\alpha}\,\epsilon^{\beta\gamma}\delta_{ij}\;,

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}q^{c}}=(\lambda^{3L}_{pr})^{\dagger}\,\bar{\ell}_{p\alpha}\,\sigma^{I}_{\alpha\gamma}\,\epsilon^{\gamma\beta}\delta_{ij}\;,

(A.55)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}q}=2(\lambda^{3\not{B}}_{pr})^{\dagger}\,\epsilon^{ijk}\,(\bar{q}^{c}_{pk}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\sigma^{I})_{\beta}\;,

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{T}q^{c}}=2(\lambda^{3\not{B}}_{pr})\,\epsilon^{ijk}\,(\bar{q}_{pk}\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot\epsilon)_{\beta}\;.

(A.56)

Matrix Structure

\displaystyle\mathbf{U_{S_{1}\ell}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}\ell^{c}}\\
U_{S_{1}\ell}&0\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{U_{S_{1}q}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}q}&U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}q^{c}}\\
U_{S_{1}q}&U_{S_{1}q^{c}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\qquad\quad

(A.57)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U_{S_{1}u}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}u}&U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}u^{c}}\\
U_{S_{1}u}&U_{S_{1}u^{c}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\quad\mathbf{U_{S_{1}d}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}d}&0\\
0&U_{S_{1}d^{c}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\quad\mathbf{U_{S_{1}e}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}e^{c}}\\
U_{S_{1}e}&0\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad

(A.58)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{1}u}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}u}&0\\
0&U_{\tilde{S}_{1}u^{c}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{1}e}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}e^{c}}\\
U_{\tilde{S}_{1}e}&0\end{pmatrix}\;.\qquad\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{1}d}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}d^{c}}\\
U_{\tilde{S}_{1}d}&\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad

(A.59)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U_{S_{2}\ell}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}\ell^{c}}\\
U_{S_{2}^{T}\ell}&0\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{U_{S_{2}d}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}q}&0\\
0&U_{S_{2}^{T}q^{c}}\end{pmatrix}\;.\qquad\qquad

(A.60)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbf{U_{S_{2}u}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}u}&0\\
0&U_{S_{2}^{T}u^{c}}\end{pmatrix}\;.\qquad\qquad\mathbf{U_{S_{2}e}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}e^{c}}\\
U_{S_{2}^{T}e}&0\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\qquad

(A.61)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{2}\ell}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}\ell^{c}}\\
U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}\ell}&0\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{U_{\tilde{S}_{2}d}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}d}&0\\
0&U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}d^{c}}\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\qquad

(A.62)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U_{S_{3}\ell}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}\ell^{c}}\\
U_{S_{3}^{T}\ell}&0\end{pmatrix}\;,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{U_{S_{3}q}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}q}&U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}q^{c}}\\
U_{S_{3}^{T}q}&U_{S_{3}^{T}q^{c}}\end{pmatrix}\;.\qquad\qquad

(A.63)

\mathbf{U_{S_{n}H}}

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}H}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H1}H_{\beta}^{\ast}S_{1i}+A_{\tilde{2}1}\,\tilde{S}_{2i\beta}^{\ast}+\lambda_{H13}^{\ast}\,S_{3i}^{I}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{I})_{\beta}\;,

(A.64)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}^{\dagger}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H1}H_{\beta}S_{1i}^{\dagger}+\lambda_{H13}^{\ast}\,S_{3i}^{I}\,(\sigma^{I}\cdot H)^{T}_{\beta}\;,

(A.65)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}H}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H1}H_{\beta}^{\ast}S_{1i}+\lambda_{H13}\,S_{3i}^{I\dagger}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{I})_{\beta}\;,

(A.66)

\displaystyle U_{S_{1}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H1}H_{\beta}S_{1i}^{\dagger}+A_{\tilde{2}1}^{\ast}\tilde{S}_{2i\beta}+\lambda_{H13}\,S_{3i}^{I\dagger}\,(\sigma^{I}\cdot H)^{T}_{\beta}\;.

(A.67)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}H}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H\tilde{1}}\,H_{\beta}^{\ast}\,\tilde{S}_{1i}-\lambda_{3\tilde{1}}\left[(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\sigma^{I})_{\beta}-(\epsilon\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot H)^{T}_{\beta}\right]S_{3i}^{I}\;,

(A.68)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}^{\dagger}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H\tilde{1}}\,H_{\beta}\,\tilde{S}_{1i}\;,

(A.69)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}H}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H\tilde{1}}\,H_{\beta}^{\ast}\,\tilde{S}_{1i}^{\dagger}\;,

(A.70)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{1}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H\tilde{1}}\,H_{\beta}\,\tilde{S}_{1i}^{\dagger}-\lambda_{3\tilde{1}}^{\ast}\,S_{3i}^{I\dagger}\left[(\sigma^{I}\cdot\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})^{T}_{\beta}-(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot\epsilon)_{\beta}\right]\;.

(A.71)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}H}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H2}\,H_{\beta}^{\ast}\,S_{2i\alpha}^{\ast}-\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}\,\delta_{\alpha\beta}(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\tilde{S}_{2i}^{\ast})-\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}H_{\alpha}(\epsilon\cdot\tilde{S}^{\ast}_{2i})^{T}_{\beta}-\lambda_{22}\,(\epsilon\cdot S_{2i}^{\ast})^{T}_{\beta}(\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})^{T}_{\alpha}\;,

(A.72)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{\dagger}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H2}\,H_{\beta}\,S_{2i\alpha}^{\ast}-\lambda_{22}\,(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot S_{2i}^{\ast})\,\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\;,

(A.73)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{T}H}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H2}H_{\beta}^{\ast}\,S_{2i\alpha}-\lambda_{22}\,\epsilon^{\beta\alpha}\,(S_{2i}^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})\;,

(A.74)

\displaystyle U_{S_{2}^{T}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H2}\,H_{\beta}\,S_{2i\alpha}+\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}^{\ast}\,\delta_{\alpha\beta}\,(\tilde{S}_{2i}\cdot\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})+\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}^{\ast}\,H_{\alpha}^{\ast}\,(\tilde{S}_{2i}^{T}\cdot\epsilon)_{\beta}-\lambda_{22}\,(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon)_{\alpha}\,(S_{2i}^{T}\cdot\epsilon)_{\beta}\;.

(A.75)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}H}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\lambda}_{H2}H_{\beta}^{\ast}\tilde{S}_{2i\alpha}+A_{\tilde{2}1}S_{1i}^{\dagger}\delta_{\alpha\beta}-A_{\tilde{2}3}\,S_{3i}^{I\dagger}\,\sigma^{I}_{\alpha\beta}-\lambda_{\tilde{2}\tilde{2}}\,\delta_{\alpha\beta}(H^{\dagger}\cdot\tilde{S}_{2i})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3}\lambda_{5}\epsilon^{ijk}\left(-2\epsilon^{\alpha\alpha_{1}}\tilde{S}_{2j\alpha_{1}}\tilde{S}_{2k\beta}+\tilde{S}_{2k}^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\tilde{S}_{2j}\delta_{\alpha\beta}\right)\;,

(A.76)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{\dagger}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\lambda}_{H2}H_{\beta}\tilde{S}_{2i\alpha}+\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}^{\ast}\,\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot S_{2i}^{\ast})+\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}^{\ast}\,S_{2i\beta}^{\ast}\,(\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})^{T}_{\alpha}-\lambda_{\tilde{2}\tilde{2}}\,\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\,(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\tilde{S}_{2i}^{\ast})\;,

(A.77)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}H}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\lambda}_{H2}H^{\ast}_{\beta}\tilde{S}_{2i\alpha}^{\ast}-\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}\,(S_{2i}^{T}\cdot H)\epsilon^{\beta\alpha}-\lambda_{2\tilde{2}}\,S_{2i\beta}\,(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon)_{\alpha}-\lambda_{\tilde{2}\tilde{2}}\,\epsilon^{\beta\alpha}\,(\tilde{S}_{2i}^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})\;,

(A.78)

\displaystyle U_{\tilde{S}_{2}^{T}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\lambda}_{H2}H_{\beta}\tilde{S}_{2i\alpha}^{\ast}+A^{\ast}_{\tilde{2}1}\delta_{\alpha\beta}S_{1i}-A_{\tilde{2}3}^{\ast}\,\sigma^{I}_{\beta\alpha}\,S_{3i}^{I}-\lambda_{\tilde{2}\tilde{2}}\,(\tilde{S}_{2i}^{\dagger}\cdot H)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3}\lambda_{5}\epsilon^{ijk}\left(-2\epsilon^{\alpha\alpha_{1}}\tilde{S}_{2j\alpha_{1}}^{\ast}\tilde{S}_{2k\beta}^{\ast}+\tilde{S}^{\dagger}_{2k}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\tilde{S}_{2j}^{\ast}\delta_{\alpha\beta}\right)\;.

(A.79)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}H}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H3}\,H_{\beta}^{\ast}\,S_{3i}^{I}+\lambda_{H13}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{I})_{\beta}\,S_{1i}-i\lambda_{\epsilon H3}\,\epsilon^{IJK}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{J})_{\beta}\,S_{3i}^{K}\;,

(A.80)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{\dagger}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H3}\,H_{\beta}\,S_{3i}^{I}+\lambda_{H13}\,(\sigma^{I}\cdot H)^{T}_{\beta}\,S_{1i}-i\lambda_{\epsilon H3}\,\epsilon^{IJK}\,(\sigma^{J}\cdot H)^{T}_{\beta}\,S_{3i}^{K}

\displaystyle+\lambda_{3\tilde{1}}\,\tilde{S}_{1i}\left[(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot\epsilon)-(\sigma^{I}\cdot\epsilon\cdot H^{\ast})^{T}\right]_{\beta}\;,

(A.81)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{T}H}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H3}\,H_{\beta}^{\ast}\,S_{3i}^{I\ast}+\lambda_{H13}^{\ast}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{I})_{\beta}\,S_{1i}^{\dagger}-i\lambda_{\epsilon H3}\,\epsilon^{IJK}\,(H^{\dagger}\cdot\sigma^{K})_{\beta}\,S_{3i}^{J\ast}

\displaystyle-\lambda_{3\tilde{1}}\,\tilde{S}_{1i}^{\dagger}\,\left[(H^{T}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\sigma^{I})-(\epsilon\cdot\sigma^{I}\cdot H)^{T}\right]_{\beta}\;,

(A.82)

\displaystyle U_{S_{3}^{T}H^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{H3}\,H_{\beta}\,S_{3i}^{I\ast}+\lambda_{H13}^{\ast}\,S_{1i}^{\dagger}\,(\sigma^{I}\cdot H)^{T}_{\beta}-i\lambda_{\epsilon H3}\,\epsilon^{IJK}\,(\sigma^{K}\cdot H)^{T}_{\beta}\,S_{3i}^{J\ast}

(A.83)

Matrix Structure

\displaystyle\mathbf{U_{S_{n}H}}=\begin{pmatrix}U_{S_{n}^{\dagger}H}&U_{S_{n}^{\dagger}H^{\ast}}\\
U_{S_{n}^{T}H}&U_{S_{n}^{T}H^{\ast}}\end{pmatrix}\;.

(A.84)

A.3
\mathbf{X_{LS}}

\mathbf{U_{fS_{n}}}

\displaystyle U_{\bar{\ell}^{c}S_{1}}=(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr})^{T}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}q_{rj\beta}\;,

\displaystyle U_{\bar{\ell}S_{1}^{\dagger}}=(\lambda^{1\text{L}}_{pr})^{\dagger}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}q^{c}_{rj\beta}\;,

(A.85)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U_{\ell u}}=\mathbf{U_{\ell\ell}}=\mathbf{U_{qe}}=\mathbf{U_{u\ell}}=\mathbf{U_{uu}}=\mathbf{U_{dd}}=\mathbf{U_{de}}=\mathbf{U_{ee}}=\mathbf{U_{eq}}=\mathbf{U_{ed}}=\mathbf{0}

(A.161)

Appendix B
Supertraces vs covariant diagrams

The construction of covariant diagrams precedes chronologically the method of Supertraces. The essential difference between these two techniques is the point at which the CDE is applied. In Supertraces one first makes superdiagrams from the distinct components of the interaction matrix
\mathbf{X}
and then applies the CDE in the resulting Supertrace. In the Covariant diagrams approach the components of the interaction matrix are split explicitly into,
heavy-only
,
heavy-light
and
light-only
contributions. In the resulting trace the CDE is applied and the log-function is ultimately Taylor expanded. Finally, from the expanded formula one reads the components of the covariant diagrams in the same manner one would read of Feynman rules and the covariant diagrams are drawn. All in all Supertraces are a more compact way to present Covariant diagrams. One can also look at
Cohen:2020fcu
for a more elaborate comparison between the two approaches.

In Tables
4
and
5
we present the relevant covariant diagrams for the Leptoquark Scalar Action. We have made combinations of matrices, and momentum insertions following the rules for constructing covariant diagrams in
Zhang:2016pja
. We have classified them in terms of
U
,
P
and
Z
insertions. In total we count 60 covariant diagrams.

Appendix C
Green Basis Operators

The dimension-5 operator has the same definition in both Green and Warsaw basis:

\boldsymbol{H^{2}\psi^{2}}

\mathcal{O}_{\nu\nu}

\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\epsilon^{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}H^{\alpha}H^{\alpha_{1}}\,\bar{\ell}^{c}_{p\beta}\,\ell_{r\beta_{1}}

Table 6:
Single dimension-5 operator giving rise to neutrino masses.

The dimension-6 operators in Green basis are listed below. Shaded ones are included in Warsaw basis too.

\boldsymbol{X^{3}}

\boldsymbol{X^{2}H^{2}}

\boldsymbol{H^{2}D^{4}}

\mathcal{O}_{3G}

f^{ABC}G_{\mu}^{A\nu}G_{\nu}^{B\rho}G_{\rho}^{C\mu}

\mathcal{O}_{HG}

G_{\mu\nu}^{A}G^{A\mu\nu}(H^{\dagger}H)

\mathcal{O}_{DH}

(D_{\mu}D^{\mu}H)^{\dagger}(D_{\nu}D^{\nu}H)

\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{3G}}

f^{ABC}\widetilde{G}_{\mu}^{A\nu}G_{\nu}^{B\rho}G_{\rho}^{C\mu}

\mathcal{O}_{H\widetilde{G}}

\widetilde{G}_{\mu\nu}^{A}G^{A\mu\nu}(H^{\dagger}H)

\boldsymbol{H^{4}D^{2}}

\mathcal{O}_{3W}

\epsilon^{IJK}W_{\mu}^{I\nu}W_{\nu}^{J\rho}W_{\rho}^{K\mu}

\mathcal{O}_{HW}

W_{\mu\nu}^{I}W^{I\mu\nu}(H^{\dagger}H)

\mathcal{O}_{H\square}

(H^{\dagger}H)\square(H^{\dagger}H)

\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{3W}}

Appendix D
Auxiliary expressions from the UOLEA

We append here expressions for the UOLEA coefficients that arise in case
of possible LQs mass degeneracies.
We split them as
f_{n}=\frac{i}{16\pi^{2}}\tilde{f}_{n}
listing only
\tilde{f}_{n}
. We also adopt the notation
\Delta^{2}_{ij}=M_{i}^{2}-M_{j}^{2}
and wherever
S_{i}=\{S_{1},\tilde{S}_{2}\}
. The coefficients then read,

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{S_{i}S_{j}S_{i}}_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{2M_{i}^{6}+M_{j}^{6}+3M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}(M_{i}^{2}-2M_{j}^{2})+M_{i}^{4}M_{j}^{2}\log M_{j}^{2}/M_{i}^{2}}{6M_{i}^{2}(\Delta_{ij})^{4}}\;,

(D.1)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{S_{i}S_{i}S_{j}}_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{i}^{4}+4M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}(1+\log M_{j}^{2}/M_{i}^{2})+2M_{j}^{4}(\log M_{j}^{2}/M_{i}^{2}-5/2)}{2(\Delta_{ij})^{4}}\;,

(D.2)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{S_{i}S_{j}}_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{i}^{4}+10M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}+M_{j}^{4}}{12(\Delta_{ij}^{2})^{4}}-\frac{M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}(M_{i}^{2}+M_{j}^{2})\log M_{i}^{2}/M_{j}^{2}}{2(\Delta_{ij}^{2})^{5}}\;,

(D.3)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{13}^{S_{i}S_{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2M_{i}^{4}+5M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}-M_{j}^{4}}{12M_{i}^{2}(\Delta^{2}_{ij})^{3}}-\frac{M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}\log M_{i}^{2}/M_{j}^{2}}{(\Delta^{2}_{ij})^{5}}\;

(D.4)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{14}^{S_{i}S_{j}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{M_{i}^{4}+10M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}M_{j}^{4}}{6(\Delta^{2}_{ij})^{4}}+\frac{M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}(M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2})\log M_{i}^{2}/M_{j}^{2}}{(\Delta_{ij}^{2})^{5}}\;,

(D.5)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{S_{i}S_{j}S_{i}S_{j}}_{17}

\displaystyle=\tilde{f}^{S_{i}S_{j}}_{17}=\frac{18M_{j}^{2}(M_{i}^{4}+M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2})-2M_{i}^{6}-34M_{j}^{6}+12(M_{j}^{4}+3M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2})\log M_{j}^{2}/M_{i}^{2}}{12M_{j}^{2}(\Delta_{ij}^{2})^{5}}\;,

(D.6)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{18}^{S_{i}S_{j}S_{i}S_{j}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{f}_{18}^{S_{i}S_{j}}=\frac{M_{i}^{2}+M_{j}^{2}}{6(\Delta_{ij}^{2})^{4}}+\frac{M_{j}^{8}-M_{i}^{8}}{12M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}(\Delta_{ij}^{2})^{5}}+\frac{M^{2}_{i}M^{2}_{j}\log M_{j}^{2}/M_{i}^{2}}{(\Delta_{ij}^{2})^{5}}\;,

(D.7)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{S_{i}S_{j}S_{i}S_{j}S_{i}S_{j}}_{19}

\displaystyle=\tilde{f}^{S_{i}S_{j}}_{19}=\frac{(M_{i}^{6}-M_{j}^{6})+9M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}(\Delta_{ij}^{2})+6M^{2}_{i}M^{2}_{j}(M_{i}^{2}+M_{j}^{2})\log M_{j}^{2}/M_{i}^{2}}{12M_{i}^{2}M_{j}^{2}(\Delta_{ij}^{2})^{5}}

(D.8)
